\section*{Game Engine - Sources and Coverage}
\chapter{Game Engine --- Sources and Coverage}\label{appendix-877738f6f4d2-game-engine-sources-and-coverage}

This is the source ledger for \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Game\%20Engine\%20-\%20From\%20First\%20Frame\%20to\%20Living\%20Worlds}{Continuous Notes/Game Engine - From First Frame to Living Worlds}. It is an audit companion, not another teaching volume. Captions, selected book passages, code snapshots, and original teaching remain different evidence types.

\section{Coverage at 6 November 2026}\label{appendix-877738f6f4d2-coverage-at-6-november-2026}

The requested exhaustive video pass remains in progress. A retrieved caption file is not the same as a reviewed video. \textbf{Full caption read} means the cleaned caption text was read; it does not mean the on-screen code or final running project was visually inspected. \textbf{Partial caption review} records selected timestamp ranges and supports claims only within those ranges. \textbf{Not reviewed} must never be silently promoted because an episode title resembles a covered topic.

Kohi's core playlist was enumerated at 395 positions. Travis's channel inventory captured 573 unique metadata IDs across the inspected feeds; 426 were selected as engine candidates, including the TypeScript Engine series. Cherno's six listed series total 234 positions; another 86 channel-video candidates expand its selected set to 320. Title-based discovery is an inclusion aid, not content analysis. Feed enumeration can omit private, removed, unlisted, or otherwise inaccessible material. These are captured scope counts, not a claim to know every unpublished source.

Kohi requests encountered HTTP 429, including after a later cooldown probe; unattempted videos remain explicitly unattempted. Some Cherno videos require sign-in/age verification and were not accessed. No authenticated-account or private Patreon content was accessed.

The research cache is \texttt{/Users/swayamsingal/.codex/source-cache/game-engine-2026-11-05/}. It retains raw inventories, available captions, hashes, reading ranges, and evidence notes. \texttt{WORK\_STATE.json} records what remains. That cache supports resumption without pretending the unfinished source pass is complete.

\textbf{Full generated-ASR read} means the complete locally generated text was read. It does not mean the audio wording was manually verified. Generated transcripts never increase the YouTube-caption or full-caption counters.

\textbf{Full generated-ASR read} means the complete locally generated text was read. It does not mean the audio wording was manually verified. Generated transcripts never increase the YouTube-caption or full-caption counters.

\section{Public source-code boundaries}\label{appendix-877738f6f4d2-public-source-code-boundaries}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Kohi GitHub mirror: revision \texttt{05a5193d5ed4890703cfcbbeb5c5e947feefecf6}. The archive transfer was truncated, so only inspected files support code claims. Kohi's official repository says SVN is now its latest source authority; this snapshot does not claim current SVN parity. \href{https://github.com/travisvroman/kohi}{Kohi repository}.
\item
  Public Hazel: revision \texttt{1feb70572fa87fa1c4ba784a2cfeada5b4a500db} from 2023. This is distinct from later video announcements and private Hazel development. \href{https://github.com/TheCherno/Hazel/tree/1feb70572fa87fa1c4ba784a2cfeada5b4a500db}{Hazel repository at the inspected revision}.
\item
  Neither complete external engine was built, run, or GPU-tested for this note. The independent CPU laboratory has its own checks below.
\end{itemize}

\section{Executable and rendering verification}\label{appendix-877738f6f4d2-executable-and-rendering-verification}

The original inline \texttt{runtime\_lab.cpp} was compiled and run with Homebrew Clang 18.1.8 using \texttt{-std=c++20\ -Wall\ -Wextra\ -Wpedantic\ -Werror}; it passed its timing-partition, interpolation, stale-handle, deferred-removal, capacity, overload, and invalid-duration assertions. The exact body also passed Apple Clang 21.0.0 with AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer. An initial Homebrew sanitizer binary did not terminate during observation and was stopped; that run is not reported as passed.

The physics numeric witness checks the stated explicit/semi-implicit/constant-acceleration results and signed impulse/momentum example. These CPU tests are limited to the saved examples. They do not validate a windowed engine, graphics API integration, a complete collision solver, audio deadlines, network behavior, or editor interactions. Final local link, fence, Mermaid, MathJax, and code-body checks are recorded in the checkpoint reports.

\section{Continuing this one volume}\label{appendix-877738f6f4d2-continuing-this-one-volume}

Keep future game-engine lessons in \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Game\%20Engine\%20-\%20From\%20First\%20Frame\%20to\%20Living\%20Worlds}{Continuous Notes/Game Engine - From First Frame to Living Worlds}. Record their original source and supplied curriculum part numbers, define any prerequisite at first useful appearance, then weave the material into the relevant explanation. Retain missing/partial evidence states. The next source pass should prioritize unreviewed available captions, then retry only genuinely missing tracks when access permits. Do not redownload successful files or overwrite a final snapshot with an older module draft.

\section{Batch 001 --- capped acquisition and deeper reading}\label{appendix-877738f6f4d2-batch-001-capped-acquisition-and-deeper-reading}

This continuation selected 50 already available caption sources for deeper review and a separate queue of at most 50 missing public captions for sequential acquisition. Eight new Kohi English captions were saved; the ninth request returned HTTP 429 and acquisition stopped immediately. The remaining 41 queue entries were not requested. Internal retries were disabled, with an eight-second gap between source requests. No authenticated or private content was accessed.

50 previously sampled sources were promoted to full-caption reads after complete cleaned-text reading. Sources not fully read retain their earlier partial status; merely putting a video into a 50-item queue does not count it as completed. The eight new captions remain not reviewed. Exact source IDs, transcript hashes, actual reading extent, acquisition logs, and before snapshots are in \texttt{batches/batch-001/} under the research cache. The merged \texttt{work/source-audit.json} governs current counts over older channel-local snapshots.

Original pixel-layout and ray-picking numerical witnesses accompany the new teaching. The saved validation report states which final checks were executed. On-screen code, external engine builds, and native whole-book Obsidian layout remain unverified.

\section{Batch 002 --- full reading and audio transcription fallback}\label{appendix-877738f6f4d2-batch-002-full-reading-and-audio-transcription-fallback}

50 previously partial sources from the saved next50 queue received full cleaned-caption reads. 49 further YouTube caption tracks were retrieved across 49 sequential caption-source requests, with an eight-second pause and no internal retries. Including the single public-audio source request, acquisition remained capped at50 sources. See \texttt{batches/batch-002/attempts.json} for exact outcomes; a request cap does not imply every source succeeded.

The subtitle-free Kohi introduction \href{https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dHPuU-DJoBM}{dHPuU-DJoBM} was processed from public audio using local OpenAI Whisper \texttt{base.en} (package20250625, CPU). The generated49-word VTT and raw model segments are retained separately from YouTube caption tracks. The entire generated text was read. The model misrecognized the project-name ending; that uncertainty is recorded and no technical claim is inferred from it. This is a full generated-text read, not manually verified transcription or visual inspection. Source audio/model/transcript hashes and exact generation configuration are preserved. \href{https://github.com/openai/whisper}{Official Whisper reference}.

Per-ID full-reading records, actual text extents, original before snapshots, generated-ASR provenance, and validation reports live in \texttt{batches/batch-002/}. Current counts come from merged \texttt{work/source-audit.json}, not older channel-local status files. The exhaustive source project remains unfinished; external engine builds, GPU/network/audio behavior, and whole-book native Obsidian layout remain untested.

\section{Batch 003 --- resumed reading, sequential acquisition, and audio ASR}\label{appendix-877738f6f4d2-batch-003-resumed-reading-sequential-acquisition-and-audio-asr}

50 incomplete saved-caption sources were promoted to full reads from the next50 queue, which originally contained29 sampled reviews and21 unread captions. One verified full-read record from an unfinished attempt was recovered rather than reread; its stored source text/hash and findings were retained. The remaining sources were read end-to-end in this continuation.

7 English YouTube caption tracks were saved across8 sequential caption-source requests, with an eight-second gap and internal retries disabled. Requests stop on the firstHTTP429. Public audio already cached for \href{https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=yBP1gSbQPPM}{ImGui Events} was reused for the audio fallback; no new request for that audio was made. The acquisition queue was capped at49 alongside that cached source. Exact source outcomes are in \texttt{batches/batch-003/attempts.json}.

Local OpenAI Whisper \texttt{base.en}, package20250625, CPU, generated 4286 words from the cached audio. The whole generated text was read. API names, code widths, and a repeated outro remain uncertain; the generated text is not a verbatim or manually audio-verified record. It is classified separately from YouTube captions. The reviewed text spans, audio/model/VTT hashes, contextual prompt, and ASR runtime parameters are preserved. The resulting teaching distinguishes historical narrated input plumbing from current primary guidance checked in the \href{https://github.com/ocornut/imgui/blob/master/docs/FAQ.md}{Dear ImGui FAQ} and \href{https://www.glfw.org/docs/latest/input_guide.html}{GLFW input guide}.

All source-reading receipts, original snapshots, acquisition and transcription logs, teaching drafts, merge scripts, numeric witnesses, and actual validation reports are in \texttt{batches/batch-003/}. The merged \texttt{work/source-audit.json} governs current counts. Caption/ASR reading does not establish on-screen-code inspection, external-engine execution, or native whole-book Obsidian/PDF layout. The exhaustive project remains incomplete.

\section{Batch 004 --- render graphs, UI geometry, and renderer ownership}\label{appendix-877738f6f4d2-batch-004-render-graphs-ui-geometry-and-renderer-ownership}

All 50 selected saved-caption sources were read end-to-end. The original queue contained 43 unread captions and seven sampled reviews. Ten unique full reads from the interrupted work were recovered using source hashes and normalized spoken-word counts; duplicate receipts were counted once. The final two sources were completed separately and reconciled against the exact original 50-source inventory.

Sequential acquisition saved 7 English YouTube tracks from 8 requests, eight seconds apart with internal retries disabled. Request eight returned HTTP 429 and acquisition stopped; 41 further queued caption sources were not requested. Public audio for \href{https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ImtWD_9OAeY}{Sparky: Ultra-Fast Batch Renderer} was fetched once and reused during the continuation. Caption and audio requests together were capped at 50 sources. Exact outcomes remain in \texttt{batches/batch-004/attempts.json} and \texttt{audio-acquisition.json}.

Local OpenAI Whisper \texttt{base.en}, package 20250625, CPU, generated 17,680 words from that audio. The complete generated text was read. Technical names, integer-width wording and narrated performance claims remain uncertain; this is generated ASR, not a YouTube caption or manually audio-verified quotation. Audio/model/transcript hashes, model parameters, contextual hint, full reading receipt and uncertainty notes remain separate. No private or authentication-restricted source was substituted.

Teaching additions are organized around engine problems, including render-pass dependencies, resource staging, rotated UI hit tests, stencil clips, shader layouts, binary resources, audio lifecycle and indexed batches. The rendergraph ordering and UI inverse-transform examples have independent numerical witnesses. Historical implementation limits and unfinished fixes remain explicit.

Reading receipts, original snapshots, acquisition/transcription logs, synthesized additions and validation reports are retained in \texttt{batches/batch-004/}. The merged \texttt{work/source-audit.json} governs current counts. Reading captions or generated text does not establish inspection of video frames, source-code correctness, engine execution, or native whole-book Obsidian/PDF layout. The exhaustive all-video project remains unfinished.

\subsection{Caption source-to-volume map}\label{appendix-877738f6f4d2-caption-source-to-volume-map}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Ep. 5.5: Unions} --- complements \texttt{\#\#\#\ Keep\ renderer\ contracts\ small\ while\ the\ backend\ grows} and \texttt{\#\#\#\ From\ vertices\ to\ useful\ render\ operations} with a historical matrix-storage representation example. Preserve the distinction between shared storage and copied return values, and qualify the union portability caveat.
\item
  \textbf{Ep. 8: Simple Renderer} --- maps chiefly to \texttt{\#\#\ Give\ the\ renderer\ a\ clear\ job,\ then\ meet\ the\ graphics\ API}, especially \texttt{\#\#\#\ Put\ a\ rendering\ boundary\ between\ games\ and\ graphics\ APIs}, \texttt{\#\#\#\#\ From\ vertices\ to\ useful\ render\ operations}, and \texttt{\#\#\#\ Keep\ submitted\ render\ resources\ alive\ until\ their\ users\ finish}. The sample illustrates submit/flush, vertex-array/index-buffer binding, indexed drawing, and a queue-consumption bug. Preserve its per-object draw-call and per-frame matrix reconstruction limits; do not present the legacy API as current Hazel behavior.
\item
  \textbf{Ep. 14: FreeImage} --- complements \texttt{\#\#\#\ Make\ build\ and\ source\ organization\ part\ of\ maintenance} and the renderer/API discussion under \texttt{\#\#\ Give\ the\ renderer\ a\ clear\ job,\ then\ meet\ the\ graphics\ API}. Map the external image library and its runtime dependency to build integration; map dimensions, bits per pixel, pitch, row direction, and channel interpretation to texture-data ingestion. Keep the exploratory debugging and legacy dependency explicit.
\item
  \textbf{Visualizing Physics Colliders} --- maps to \texttt{\#\#\#\ Keep\ the\ standard\ UI\ optional\ and\ stateful,\ but\ name\ its\ current\ limits} for the settings toggle, \texttt{\#\#\#\ Keep\ example\ behavior\ in\ the\ example\ application} for editor/runtime separation, and \texttt{\#\#\ Give\ the\ renderer\ a\ clear\ job,\ then\ meet\ the\ graphics\ API} for overlay rendering. Preserve the concrete transform construction and the unresolved opposite-side circle visibility issue.
\item
  \textbf{Ep. 4: Vectors} --- maps to the volume's math and coordinate-space material, especially \texttt{\#\#\#\ Camera\ and\ coordinate\ spaces} and the graphics material under \texttt{\#\#\ Give\ the\ renderer\ a\ clear\ job,\ then\ meet\ the\ graphics\ API}. It supplies a historical vector value-semantics example; preserve the final fixes to non-mutating operators and avoid implying this source covers later direction math.
\item
  \textbf{Ep. 24: Cleaning Up and Making a Game} --- maps to \texttt{\#\#\#\ Make\ build\ and\ source\ organization\ part\ of\ maintenance}, \texttt{\#\#\#\ Keep\ example\ behavior\ in\ the\ example\ application}, \texttt{\#\#\#\ Keep\ engine\ lifecycle\ separate\ from\ game-specific\ frame\ work}, and \texttt{\#\#\#\ Packaging\ changes\ the\ host,\ not\ the\ engine’s\ obligations}. Capture the feature freeze, extraction of game-specific tile behavior, application-loop sketch, texture batch limits, and portability intent; explicitly note that Emscripten completion is not shown.
\item
  \textbf{Ep. 17: Texture Arrays} --- maps to \texttt{\#\#\#\#\ From\ vertices\ to\ useful\ render\ operations} and \texttt{\#\#\#\ Keep\ renderer\ contracts\ small\ while\ the\ backend\ grows}. The source actually uses a list of ordinary 2D texture bindings and per-vertex sampler slots, not a GPU texture-array object. Preserve the untextured color fallback, flush-on-capacity design, legacy slot count, and untested overflow path.
\item
  \textbf{Ep. 10: Optimization and Maintenance} --- maps to \texttt{\#\#\#\ Make\ build\ and\ source\ organization\ part\ of\ maintenance}, \texttt{\#\#\#\#\ API\ abstraction\ has\ a\ price}, \texttt{\#\#\#\#\ From\ vertices\ to\ useful\ render\ operations}, and the existing matrix/allocator correctness material. Preserve the profiling method, relative-percent caveat, hard-coded-offset replacement, packed-color experiment, and temporary-buffer matrix fix; historical FPS numbers are not transferable benchmarks.
\item
  \textbf{Ep. 12: Transformation Stack} --- maps to \texttt{\#\#\#\ Camera\ and\ coordinate\ spaces} and \texttt{\#\#\#\#\ From\ vertices\ to\ useful\ render\ operations}. It adds hierarchical object transforms and homogeneous position multiplication. Preserve transform composition order as version-specific, the identity-preserving pop guard, and the unresolved need for grouped submissions in the next episode.
\item
  \textbf{Playing and Stopping Scenes (and Resetting)} --- maps to \texttt{\#\#\#\ Keep\ example\ behavior\ in\ the\ example\ application}, \texttt{\#\#\ Keep\ identity,\ storage,\ and\ ownership\ in\ agreement}, and the scene serialization/build material. The key contribution is copy-on-play scene isolation with UUID remapping and component transfer. Preserve the manually maintained component list and the reference-invalidation/debugging corrections as version-specific unfinished concerns.
\item
  \textbf{Can AI Make a Game Engine?} --- maps to \texttt{\#\#\ Know\ which\ story\ each\ source\ can\ tell} and \texttt{\#\#\#\#\ Source-to-volume\ map}. It is evidence about a narrated prototype demonstration and its experiment setup, not inspected implementation quality. Keep the unequal run conditions, unresolved provenance, narrator's pending code-review caveat, missing Forge controls, and bounded smoke-test claims explicit.
\item
  \textbf{Community Issues/PRs and Merging Branches} --- maps to \texttt{\#\#\ Know\ which\ story\ each\ source\ can\ tell}, \texttt{\#\#\#\#\ Source-to-volume\ map}, and \texttt{\#\#\#\ Make\ build\ and\ source\ organization\ part\ of\ maintenance}. It documents branch integration, component-copy coverage, SDK/debug dependency changes, and scope-based contribution triage. Preserve that the keyboard-layout design remains unresolved and the renderer rewrite is future work in this episode.
\item
  \textbf{Everything You Need in a 2D Game Engine (Hazel 2D)} --- maps to \texttt{\#\#\#\ Put\ a\ rendering\ boundary\ between\ games\ and\ graphics\ APIs}, \texttt{\#\#\#\ Add\ scripting\ at\ a\ deliberate\ boundary}, \texttt{\#\#\#\ Integrate\ physics\ without\ surrendering\ the\ scene\ model}, \texttt{\#\#\#\ Build\ the\ editor\ around\ inspectable\ operations}, \texttt{\#\#\#\ Keep\ an\ audio\ front\ end\ separate\ from\ its\ playback\ backend}, \texttt{\#\#\ Prepare\ and\ publish\ content\ before\ promising\ it\ is\ ready}, \texttt{\#\#\#\ Generate\ platform\ projects\ from\ one\ configuration,\ then\ verify\ each\ target}, \texttt{\#\#\#\ Read\ performance\ results\ with\ the\ workload\ attached}, and \texttt{\#\#\#\ Packaging\ changes\ the\ host,\ not\ the\ engine’s\ obligations}. This source is a Hazel 2D roadmap, not an implementation report: its explicit 2D focus, Hollow Knight-style target, and stretch goals are intentions. Preserve the roadmap as plans and do not convert them into completed implementation claims.
\item
  \textbf{Reading/Writing C\# Fields in Editor} --- maps to \texttt{\#\#\#\ Scripts\ need\ a\ host,\ a\ lifecycle,\ and\ inspectable\ state}, \texttt{\#\#\#\ Treat\ editor\ panels\ as\ clients\ of\ live\ engine\ state}, and \texttt{\#\#\#\ Save\ scenes\ as\ a\ versioned\ authoring\ contract}. Distinguish class-level field metadata from per-instance values, and mark edit-time persistence as deferred; only float controls are implemented in this episode.
\item
  \textbf{Introduction to C++: Sparky Engine} --- maps to \texttt{\#\#\#\ Make\ build\ and\ source\ organization\ part\ of\ maintenance}, \texttt{\#\#\#\#\ Precompiled\ headers\ as\ a\ measured\ project\ choice}, \texttt{\#\#\#\ Make\ build\ and\ source\ organization\ part\ of\ maintenance}, and \texttt{\#\#\ Keep\ identity,\ storage,\ and\ ownership\ in\ agreement}. It is a historical language orientation for later Sparky episodes; preserve the platform-specific width caveat and the introductory limits around raw arrays and manual allocation.
\item
  \textbf{C\# Scripting!} --- maps to \texttt{\#\#\#\ Scripts\ need\ a\ host,\ a\ lifecycle,\ and\ inspectable\ state}, \texttt{\#\#\#\ Add\ scripting\ at\ a\ deliberate\ boundary}, and \texttt{\#\#\#\ Managed\ scripts\ extend\ the\ host,\ not\ the\ engine\textquotesingle{}s\ storage\ model}. It adds a historical Mono embedding path: runtime/app-domain setup, managed assembly search paths, reflection for types, managed object construction, and native-to-managed method invocation. Preserve the Windows/Mono/Premake version context and the unresolved teardown race; entity integration, native internal calls, persistent authored values, and a complete editor workflow remain future work.
\end{itemize}

This pass adds a cross-source synthesis in \texttt{additions-b.md}; it does not duplicate the concepts already taught in the continuous volume. The headings below are exact headings in \texttt{Continuous\ Notes/Game\ Engine\ -\ From\ First\ Frame\ to\ Living\ Worlds.md} and identify the existing teaching that already covers each source's core. The additions note contributes the narrower observations recorded in the third column: boundary failures, integration paths, and evidence limits.

\section{Coverage gaps preserved}\label{appendix-877738f6f4d2-coverage-gaps-preserved}

The caption set contains demonstrations and discussion for legacy OpenGL, early Hazel/Sparky input, textures, shaders, fonts, groups, audio setup, C\# scripting, and editor workflow. It does not establish current behavior for any named API or engine branch. Several episodes openly end with unfinished work or failed experiments. No video-frame evidence or manual audio inspection is claimed. These sources extend the book's teaching examples and provenance notes; they do not replace its present-day API boundaries, measured benchmarks, or implementation evidence.

The source materials are historical, narrated, and at times incomplete; the synthesis labels plans and creator/reviewer observations accordingly. The Unreal reaction and creator strategy discussions are contextual observations rather than engine implementation evidence.

\section{Limits}\label{appendix-877738f6f4d2-limits}

All eleven group B caption texts were fully read, with canonical word counts and cleaned-file hashes saved in \texttt{reviews-b.json}. This is not frame-by-frame review, source checkout, build reproduction, benchmark verification, or current API research. Some videos are reactions, code reviews, or historic roadmap reports; opinions, narrated observations, and confirmed mechanisms are distinguished in their receipts. The coverage map routes all salient findings, but it is not a literal claim that every utterance has a unique paragraph in the book. Several foundations are already covered under the exact headings above; additions-b.md therefore prioritizes the incremental cold-start investigation, grid-sizing derivation, ownership decision questions, and evidence boundary.

\emph{Game Engine Architecture} 784--785 / 762--763 & A subsystem-oriented frame loop; cache-friendly contiguous subsystem data; once-per-batch global work; reusable frame resources; pipelined processing; coupled multi-body collision resolution as group work & ``Let dependencies set the update order'' \\
GEA 785--788 / 763--766 & Parent/held-object ordering; animation-to-ragdoll and ragdoll-to-animation data flow; subsystem phases; pre/post hooks; multiple per-frame object hooks; per-phase membership lists to avoid useless iteration & ``Let dependencies set the update order'' \\
GEA 788--790 / 766--768 & Dependency forest and tiers; root-first buckets; full phased subsystem passes per bucket; shallow-depth examples; external systems may not accept bucketed calls; the author's historical \emph{Uncharted} account & ``Let dependencies set the update order'' \\
GEA 790--793 / 768--771 & Sequential partial updates; consistent states only before/after a loop; wrong-time reads and one-frame lag; old/new state caching and interpolation; memory cost and residual inconsistency; time stamps and same-bucket query guards & ``Make mixed-time reads visible'' \\
GEA 793--795 / 771--773 & Object dependency makes parallel updates hard; direct state peeking vs message passing; serial high-level model and parallel low-level systems as the author's account; blocking vs non-blocking requests; issue as early as possible and consume as late as possible; tolerable stale results; batch ray requests; page 795 introduces events and notification/response duties & ``Make mixed-time reads visible'' \\
\emph{Game Physics Engine Development} 268--272 / 245--249 & Insertion descends by child \texttt{getGrowth}; leaf split into existing/new child; ancestor-bound refresh; enclosing but nonminimal sphere; reason for runtime approximation; deletion replaces parent with sibling data, detaches adopted children, and recalculates upward; apparent double-delete typo preserved as a warning & ``Update the hierarchy without rebuilding all of it'' \\
Physics 273--274 / 250--251 & Multiple bounds for awkward sub-objects; level decomposition; box bounds for boxlike spaces; transition from object-dependent hierarchy to world-space partition & ``Grow a spatial hierarchy around the world's shape''; ``Update the hierarchy without rebuilding all of it'' \\
Physics 274--278 / 251--255 & Spatial structure is fixed to the world while a BVH follows its objects; hybrid combinations; BSP plane representation and signed dot-product side test; normalized direction and sphere crossing; nodes/leaves can contain sets; duplicate cross-plane object policy and candidate pairs; optional BVH per leaf; static-level collision leaves; preprocessing cost & ``Grow a spatial hierarchy around the world's shape'' \\
Physics 278--279 / 255--256 & Quad-tree four-way ground/2D split; oct-tree eight-way 3D split; fixed world axes; coordinate-to-child selection with the source's example; memory reason for choosing quad-tree in ground-focused 3D; apparent uninitialized child index in printed excerpt & ``Grow a spatial hierarchy around the world's shape'' \\
\end{longtable}
}

The merged \texttt{pdf-additions.md} additionally contains the separately authored scaled sphere-gap visual and recall prompt. It illustrates the already derived parent/child bound example and makes no claim about new book content. Grids are named as a common spatial structure on PDF p.~274 but their mechanics are outside the assigned range; the teaching text labels that limit. Event dispatch is introduced only at the end of GEA p.~795, so the draft records notification and response responsibilities without inventing a queue design.

Read receipts are in \texttt{pdf-assignment-a.json} and \texttt{pdf-reviews-a.json} for the technical pages, with combined four-book receipts in \texttt{pdf-assignment.json} and \texttt{pdf-reviews.json}. The C pages and scaled visual have separate source drafts. This ledger does not establish whole-book coverage, code correctness, runtime behavior, or comparative performance.
